x\in\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})
and
v_{k}\in\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}(x)
,
h_{k}^{x}=h_{k}^{D}
.

Proof.

For ease of notation, in this proof we work with unscaled markets
z:V\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
which we treat like distributions without imposing

\sum_{k}z_{k}=1
. We only require that for all
k
,

\sum_{z}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}(z)z_{k}=x^{\ast}_{k}.

(A.1)

At the end, we can convert these unscaled markets into actual markets by
re-scaling:

x_{k}=\frac{z_{k}}{\sum_{i}z_{i}},\quad\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}(x)={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}(z)\cdot\sum_{i}z_{i}.

This rescaling does not affect the hazard rates (and hence the implied
allocations) in any way. Additionally,

\sum_{k}\left[\sum_{z}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}(z)z_{k}\right]=1\implies\sum_{k}\left[\sum_{x}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}(x)x_{k}\right]=1\implies\sum_{x}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}(x)=1

so
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}
is in fact a valid segmentation.

Let
D
be the function we want to implement. This proof works by
induction, going decreasing down the support. Start with some segmentation
\textstyle\mathchar 283
over
\{z^{1},\dots,z^{L}\}
with the property that for some value
j
,
for all
k\leq j
,

z^{\ell}_{k}=x^{\ast}_{k}.

(A.2)

Furthermore, for all
k>j
and
z_{\ell}
such that
v_{k}\in\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}(z_{\ell})
,

h_{k}^{\ell}=\frac{\Delta_{k}^{\ell}D^{\ell}_{k+1}}{z^{\ell}_{k}}=h_{k}^{D}=\frac{(v_{k+1}-v_{k})D_{k+1}}{x^{\ast}_{k}}.

(A.3)

(
A.1
)-(
A.3
) are clearly satisfied by the trivial
segmentation for
j=K-1
. We produce a segmentation
\hat{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}
supported
on at most one extra market which preserves (
A.1
)-(
A.3
)
at
j-1
.

The construction is as follows. Suppose that we keep the same markets
z^{\ell}
and weights
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}(z^{\ell})
, but we modify them only at
v_{j}
so
that either (
A.3
) holds, or
z^{\ell}_{j}=0
. That is,

\hat{z}^{\ell}_{j}=\begin{cases}\frac{h_{j}^{\ell}}{h_{j}^{D}}\cdot x^{\ast}_{j}&v_{j}\in\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}(\hat{z}_{\ell})\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

Since
\hat{z}_{k}^{\ell}=z_{k}^{\ell}
for all
k\neq j
, (
A.1
)-(
A.2
) continue to hold except at
k=j
. Clearly, if we remove
v_{j}
from every market, then
\sum_{\ell}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}(z^{\ell})\hat{z}^{\ell}_{j}=0<x^{\ast}_{j}
. We claim that if
v_{j}
is included in the support of every
market, then

\sum_{\ell}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}(z^{\ell})\hat{z}^{\ell}_{j}\geq x^{\ast}_{j}.

To prove this, we first note that:

\sum_{\ell}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}(z^{\ell})\hat{z}^{\ell}_{j}=\sum_{\ell}\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}(z^{\ell})h_{j}^{\ell}}{h_{j}^{D}}\cdot x^{\ast}_{j}=\frac{h^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}_{j}}{h^{D}_{j}}\cdot x^{\ast}_{j}=\frac{D^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}_{j+1}}{D_{j+1}}\cdot x^{\ast}_{j}.

We now show that

\frac{D^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}_{j+1}}{D_{j+1}}\geq 1.

Observe that
v_{j}
is in the support of all
z^{\ell}
. Thus, by (
32
) and (
A.1
),

\sum_{i=j}^{K-1}(v_{i+1}-v_{i})D^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}_{j+1}=\sum_{i=j}^{K-1}(v_{i+1}-v_{i})D^{\ast}_{j+1}.

(A.4)

The majorization constraint implies that:

\sum_{i=j}^{K-1}(v_{i+1}-v_{i})D^{\ast}_{j+1}\geq\sum_{i=j}^{K-1}(v_{i+1}-v_{i})D_{i+1}.

(A.5)

Combining (
A.4
) and (
A.5
), we have

(v_{j+1}-v_{j})D^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}_{j+1}+\sum_{i=j+1}^{K-1}(v_{i+1}-v_{i})D^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}_{i+1}\geq\sum_{i=j}^{K-1}(v_{i+1}-v_{i})D_{i+1}.

(A.6)

Furthermore, for every
k>j
, by (
A.3
)

D^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}_{k+1}=\frac{x^{\ast}_{k}h_{k}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}{v_{k+1}-v_{k}}=\frac{x^{\ast}_{k}h_{k}^{D}}{v_{k+1}-v_{k}}=D_{k+1}

and hence (
A.6
) implies that

D^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}_{j+1}\geq D_{j+1}.

Clearly, the value of
\sum_{\ell}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}(z^{\ell})z^{\ell}_{j}
is strictly decreasing as we
remove
v_{j}
from the support of markets one by one. The analysis above
shows that as some point, we cross over
x^{\ast}_{j}
. At the crossing point
we split the market into two segments, with appropriate weights so that (
A.1
) is satisfied with equality. This completes the construction
of
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}^{\prime}
.

There are two important things to point out from this proof. First, the
number of total segments is at most
K
. Second, the proof is agnostic about
the order in which we remove
v_{j}
from the support of the markets in each
step. In particular, we can order them in a way that produces markets with
more natural supports. For example, if we always order the markets by
whether they include
v_{j+1}
, we get the feature that at every
v_{j}
, either every market with a gap at
v_{j+1}
also has a gap at
v_{j}
, or
every market with
v_{j+1}
in the support also has
v_{j}
in the support.
Together, these imply that the construction has a well-defined limit when
V

approaches a continuum of values.

In the end, we are left with a segmentation such that in every market,

U(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}x^{\ast}_{k}u_{k}(h^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}_{k}).

However, we want to achieve the concavified value
\overline{u}_{k}
, not
u_{k}
. Take any segment
x
and
v_{k}
such that

\overline{u}_{k}(h_{k}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}u_{k}(h_{1})+(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})u_{k}(h_{2}),\quad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}h_{1}+(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})h_{2}=h_{k}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}},

where
h_{1}<h_{k}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}<h_{2}
.

Let us segment
x
into two markets
x^{1},x^{2}
with the same support as
x

such that: (1) for all
v_{j}\neq v_{k}
,
h_{j}^{1}=h_{j}^{2}=h_{j}^{x}
, and
(2)
h_{j}^{1}=h_{1}^{x}
,
h_{j}^{2}=h_{2}^{x}
. Since demands are equal up to
v_{k}
,
x_{k}^{1}>x>x_{k}^{2}
. Hence, there exists some
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}\in[0,1]

such that
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}x_{k}^{1}+(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}})x_{k}^{2}=x
. The payoff across this
segmentation is

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}x_{k}^{1}u_{k}(h_{1})+(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}})x_{k}^{2}u_{k}(h_{2})=\overline{u}_{k}(h_{k}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})

as desired.

The last step of the proof is to check that this constructed segmentation
respects the regularity assumption necessary for (
25
) to hold.
To do so, we rely on the following lemma.

Lemma A.2 (Submodularity Implies Regularity)

If
u_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k})=\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k})\leq\overline{u}_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k+1})=u_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k+1})
, then
v_{k+1}-h_{k+1}\geq v_{k}-h_{k}
.

Proof.

Decompose the difference

0\leq\overline{u}_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k+1})-\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k})=\big[\overline{u}_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k+1})-\overline{u}_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k}+(v_{k+1}-v_{k}))\big]+\\
\big[\overline{u}_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k}+(v_{k+1}-v_{k}))-\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k})\big].

We claim that the second bracketed difference is negative. If so, then the
first difference must be positive, which by concavity is true and only if

h_{k+1}\leq(h_{k}+(v_{k+1}-v_{k}))\iff v_{k+1}-h_{k+1}\geq v_{k}-h_{k}.

To prove this, consider the analytical extension of
u_{k}
onto all
v
:

u_{v}(h)\triangleq hQ(v-h)\implies u_{v}^{\prime}(h)=Q(v-h)-hQ^{\prime}(v-h).

Let
h(v)=h_{k}+(v-v_{k})
, so that

\overline{u}_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k}+(v_{k+1}-v_{k}))-\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k})=\int_{v_{k}}^{v_{k+1}}\frac{d}{dv}\big[\overline{u}_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))\big]\ dv.

(A.7)

There are two distinct cases: when
u_{v}(h)=\overline{u}_{v}(h)
, and where

u_{v}(h)<\overline{u}_{v}(h)
. First, for any open region where
u_{v}(h(v))=\overline{u}_{v}(h(v))
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dv}\big[\overline{u}_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dv}\big[u_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))\big]=(1-2h^{\prime}(v))Q^{\prime}(v-h)-h(v)(1-h^{\prime}(v))Q^{\prime\prime}(v-h)

\displaystyle=-Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))-(1-h^{\prime}(v))u_{v}^{\prime\prime}(h)=-Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))\leq 0,

where the second line uses that

u_{v}^{\prime\prime}(h)=hQ^{\prime\prime}(v-h)-2Q^{\prime}(v-h).

Next, we claim that for any differentiable
h(v)
,

u_{v}(h(v))<\overline{u}_{v}(h(v))\implies\frac{d}{dv}\big[\overline{u}_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))\big]\leq 0.

To see this, let
T(v)
and
B(v)
be the top and bottom support points of
\overline{u}_{v}(h(v))
, respectively, so that

\overline{u}_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))=u_{v}^{\prime}(T(v))=u_{v}^{\prime}(B(v))=\frac{u_{v}(T(v))-u_{v}(B(v))}{T(v)-B(v)}.

(A.8)

We can compute

\frac{d}{dv}\big[\overline{u}_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))\big]=\frac{u_{v}^{\prime}(T(v))\cdot T^{\prime}(v)+\frac{\partial}{\partial v}u_{v}(T(v))-u_{v}^{\prime}(B(v))\cdot B^{\prime}(v)-\frac{\partial}{\partial v}u_{v}(B(v))}{T(v)-B(v)}+\\
\frac{(T^{\prime}(v)-B^{\prime}(v))\cdot(u_{v}(T(v))-u_{v}(B(v)))}{(T(v)-B(v))^{2}}.

Substituting in (
A.8
), the second term cancels with part of the
first, resulting in

\frac{d}{dv}\big[\overline{u}_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))\big]=\frac{1}{T(v)-B(v)}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial v}u_{v}(T(v))-\frac{\partial}{\partial v}u_{v}(B(v))\right).

We can simplify this by using

\frac{\partial}{\partial v}u_{v}(h)=hQ^{\prime}(v-h).

But, note that

u_{v}^{\prime}(T(v))=Q(v-T(v))-T(v)Q^{\prime}(v-T(v))=Q(v-B(v))-B(v)Q^{\prime}(v-B(v))=u_{v}^{\prime}(B(v)).

This, in turn, means that

\frac{d}{dv}u_{v}(T(v))-\frac{\partial}{\partial v}u_{v}(B(v))=\frac{1}{T(v)-B(v)}\Big(Q(v-T(v))-Q(v-B(v))\Big)\leq 0.

Hence, the integrand of (
A.7
) is weakly negative on any open
interval, completing the proof.

To see how we can use this lemma, note that the KKT conditions for (
29
) are:

\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})=\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}_{i}

(A.9)

where
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}_{i}
is the multiplier associated with the majorization constraint
starting at
i
. All
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}_{i}\geq 0
, so at the optimal
D^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}
,
\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})\leq\overline{u}_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k+1}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})
. But, by construction, for all
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})
,
h_{k}^{x}\in\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}(\overline{u}_{k}(h_{k}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}))
. In particular, this means that

u_{k}
is concave at
h_{k}^{x}
and
u_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{x})=\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})
. Thus, we can apply Lemma
A.2
, implying
that the segmentation we constructed in Lemma
A.1
is regular,
completing the proof of Theorem
1
.

Proof of Proposition
3
.

Recall that for any
x\in\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})
and
v_{k}\in\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}(x)
,

\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{x})=\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}})

which is constant across
x
. By Lemma
A.2
, this means that for
all
k
and
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathop{\mathrm{s}upp}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})
,

v_{k+1}-h_{k+1}^{x}\geq v_{k}-h_{k}^{x^{\prime}}.

This means

q^{x}_{k+1}=Q(v_{k+1}-h_{k+1}^{x})\geq Q(v_{k}-h_{k}^{x^{\prime}})=q^{x^{\prime}}_{k}

for all
x,x^{\prime}
and instances of
v_{k},v_{k+1}
.

Proof of Lemma
6
.

Recall that

u_{k}^{\prime}(h)=Q(v_{k}-h)-hQ^{\prime}(v_{k}-h),\quad u_{k}^{\prime\prime}(h)=hQ^{\prime\prime}(v_{k}-h)-2Q^{\prime}(v_{k}-h).

u_{k}
being increasing at
h
means that

u_{k}^{\prime}(h)\geq 0\implies h\leq\frac{Q(v_{k}-h)}{Q^{\prime}(v_{k}-h)}.

We would like this to be a sufficient condition for
u_{k}
to be concave at

h
, that is, either
Q^{\prime\prime}(v_{k}-h)\leq 0
, or

u_{k}^{\prime\prime}(h)\leq 0\implies h\leq\frac{2Q^{\prime}(v_{k}-h)}{Q^{\prime\prime}(v_{k}-h)}.

A sufficient condition is that

\frac{Q(v_{k}-h)}{Q^{\prime}(v_{k}-h)}\leq\frac{2Q^{\prime}(v_{k}-h)}{Q^{\prime\prime}(v_{k}-h)}\iff\frac{Q^{\prime\prime}(v_{k}-h)Q(v_{k}-h)}{Q^{\prime}(v_{k}-h)^{2}}\leq 2.

Defining
q=Q(v_{k}-h)
, then recalling that
Q({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}})=(c^{\prime})^{-1}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}})
and applying the inverse function theorem, yields

\frac{c^{\prime\prime\prime}(q)q}{c^{\prime\prime}(q)}\geq-2

which is obviously implied by (
38
).

Proof of Lemma
7
.

This proof consists of two parts. First, we show that
\overline{h}_{k}
is
increasing while
h_{k}^{\ast}
is decreasing, meaning that for some
threshold
\widehat{k}
,
k<\widehat{k}\implies\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})=0
, and for
k\geq\widehat{k}
,
\overline{u}_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})=u_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})
.
\overline{h}_{k}
is the
discretization of

h(v)=\argmax_{h}\big[hQ(v-h)\big].

The FOC characterizing
h(v)
is

q(v-h(v))-h(v)Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))=0

Applying the implicit function theorem, we get

Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))(1-h^{\prime}(v))-h^{\prime}(v)Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))-h(v)Q^{\prime\prime}(v-h(v))(1-h^{\prime}(v))=0\\
\implies h^{\prime}(v)=1-\frac{Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))}{2Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))-h(v)Q^{\prime\prime}(v-h(v))}.

Under (
38
),

h(v)\leq\frac{Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))}{\left|Q^{\prime\prime}(v-h(v))\right|}\implies 2-\frac{h(v)Q^{\prime\prime}(v-h(v))}{Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))}\geq 1\implies h^{\prime}(v)\in[0,1].

Hence,
\overline{h}_{k}
is increasing, and so is
v_{k}-\overline{h}_{k}

(a fact we need for the proof of Proposition
5
).

Next, we show that
u_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})
is increasing for all
k\geq\widehat{k}
. We can decompose

u_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k+1}^{\ast})-u_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})=\big(u_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})-u_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})\big)+\big(u_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k+1}^{\ast})-u_{k+1}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})\big).

The first term is equal to

\int_{v_{k}}^{v_{k+1}}\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\big[u_{v}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})\big]\ dv=\int_{v_{k}}^{v_{k+1}}Q^{\prime}(v-h_{k}^{\ast})-h_{k}^{\ast}Q^{\prime\prime}(v-h_{k}^{\ast})\ dv.

Under (
38
), for any
h
such that
h\leq\overline{h}_{k}
,

hQ^{\prime\prime}(v-h)\leq Q^{\prime}(v-h)\implies\int_{v_{k}}^{v_{k+1}}\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\big[u_{v}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})\big]\ dv\geq 0.

This also means, in particular, that
u_{k+1}^{\prime}(h)\geq 0
for all
h\in(h_{k+1}^{x},h_{k}^{x})
. The second term is

\int_{h_{k}^{x}}^{h_{k+1}^{\ast}}u_{k+1}^{\prime\prime}(h)\ dh\geq 0

using the fact that
h_{k}^{\ast}\leq h_{k+1}^{\ast}
by (
39
)
and that, by Lemma
6
,
k\geq\widehat{k}\implies u_{k+1}^{\prime\prime}(h)\geq 0
. Thus,
u_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{\ast})

is increasing in
k
.

Proof of Proposition
5
.

What remains is to show that there exists a solution in which every segment
x

is supported on
\{v_{j},\dots,v_{K}\}
for some
j\leq\hat{k}
. Consider
the following demand
\hat{D}
supported on
V
, with hazard rates:

\hat{h}_{k}=\min\big\{\overline{h}_{k},h_{k}^{\ast}\big\}.

Since
\overline{h}_{k}
is increasing and
h_{k}^{\ast}
is decreasing,
\hat{h}

switches from the first argument to the second exactly once, and
\overline{h}_{k}-h_{k}^{\ast}=(v_{k}-h_{k}^{\ast})-(v_{k}-\overline{h}_{k})
satisfies
increasing differences. Furthermore, from the proof of Lemma
7
,
we know that
v_{k}-\overline{h}_{k}
is increasing, so
\hat{D}
is regular.

We are now ready to construct the segmentation. Define the family of
distributions

\hat{D}^{j}(v)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }v<v_{j};\\
\frac{\hat{D}(v)}{\hat{D}}&\text{if }v\geq v_{j}.\end{cases}

Since
\hat{D}
is regular, so is each
\hat{D}^{j}
. What we now need is to
find weights
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{j}
such that
\sum_{j}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{j}\hat{D}^{j}=D^{\ast}
and
\sum_{j}\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{j}=1
. We construct these weights
recursively.

Begin by making
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}
the largest value until
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{D}^{1}(v)\leq D^{\ast}(v)
is binding for some
v
. Our previous argument
shows that
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}_{k}^{\ast}-(v_{k}-h_{k})
is decreasing. This is the amount of
“over-weighting” in
\hat{D}
relative to
D^{\ast}
, meaning that
\frac{\hat{x}/\hat{D}}{x^{\ast}_{k}/D^{\ast}_{k}}
is decreasing, so the
binding
v
for
\hat{D}^{1}
is, in fact,
v_{1}
.

Now, consider the remainder distribution
\widetilde{D}=D^{\ast}-\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{D}^{1}
. Notice that

\frac{\hat{x}_{k}/\hat{D}_{k}}{\widetilde{x}_{k}/\widetilde{D}_{k}}=\frac{\hat{x}_{k}/\hat{D}_{k}}{(x^{\ast}_{k}-\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{x}_{k}^{1})/(D^{\ast}_{k}-\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{D}_{k}^{1})}.

Compute

\frac{x^{\ast}_{k}-\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{x}_{k}^{1}}{D^{\ast}_{k}-\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{D}_{k}^{1}}=\left[\frac{1-\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{x}_{k}^{1}/x^{\ast}_{k}}{1-\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{D}_{k}^{1}/D^{\ast}_{k}}\right]\frac{x^{\ast}_{k}}{D^{\ast}_{k}}.

Combining these two together, we get that

\frac{\hat{x}_{k}/\hat{D}_{k}}{\widetilde{x}_{k}/\widetilde{D}_{k}}=\left[\frac{1-\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{x}_{k}^{1}/x^{\ast}_{k}}{1-\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}\hat{D}_{k}^{1}/D^{\ast}_{k}}\right]^{-1}\cdot\frac{\hat{x}_{k}/\hat{D}_{k}}{x^{\ast}_{k}/D^{\ast}_{k}}.

Now,
\frac{\hat{x}_{k}/\hat{D}_{k}}{x^{\ast}_{k}/D^{\ast}_{k}}
is
decreasing in
k
, which means that the bracketed term is increasing in
k
,
and hence its inverse is also decreasing. Thus,
\frac{\widetilde{x}_{k}/\widetilde{D}_{k}}{x^{\ast}_{k}/D^{\ast}_{k}}
is overall decreasing in
k
.

We now repeat the same construction on
\widetilde{D}
, and again until we
reach
v_{\hat{k}}
, the crossing point of
\overline{h}_{k}
and
h_{k}^{\ast}
,
at which point we set
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{\hat{k}}
to be all remaining weight.
This completes the construction of
\hat{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{j}
, proving the proposition.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

The proof mirrors that of Theorem
1
. We first apply Lemma
A.1
, so all we need to do is to show that the
D
solving the
maximization problem can be implemented by a segmentation over regular
markets. The KKT conditions on
h_{k}^{D}
imply that

\frac{d}{dh}[\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{k,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}})]=\frac{d}{dh}[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{k,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}})]={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}w_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}})+(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})u_{k}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}})

is increasing in
k
. As before, decompose

\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{k+1,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k+1})-\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{k,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k})=\big[\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{k+1,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k+1})-\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{k+1,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k}+(v_{k+1}-v_{k}))\big]+\\
\big[\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{k+1,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k}+(v_{k+1}-v_{k}))-\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{k,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k})\big].

We wish to show that the second bracketed term is negative. Again, take
h(v)=h_{k}+(v-v_{k})
, so that

\big[\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{k+1,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k}+(v_{k+1}-v_{k}))-\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{k,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h_{k})\big]=\int_{v_{k}}^{v_{k+1}}\frac{d}{dv}\big[\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h(v))\big]\ dv

where
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}
is the continuous extension of
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{k,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}
onto all
v
and similarly for
w_{v}(h)
. Note that

w_{v}^{\prime}(h)=-vQ^{\prime}(v-h)+c^{\prime}(Q(v-h))Q^{\prime}(v-h)=-hQ^{\prime}(v-h).

Taking the total differential with respect to
v
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dv}\big[w_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))\big]

\displaystyle=-h^{\prime}(v)Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))-h(v)(1-h^{\prime}(v))Q^{\prime\prime}(v-h(v))

\displaystyle=-Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))-(1-h^{\prime}(v))w_{v}^{\prime\prime}(h(v))=-Q^{\prime}(v-h(v))\leq 0.

Thus, in any open interval where
\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h(v))={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h(v))
,

\frac{d}{dv}\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h(v))={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\frac{d}{dv}w_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))+(1-2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})\frac{d}{dv}u_{v}^{\prime}(h(v))\leq 0.

On the other hand, whenever
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}(h(v))<\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}(h(v))
, we have that

\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h(v))=\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}(T(v))-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}(B(v))}{T(v)-B(v)}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\cdot\frac{w_{v}(T(v))-w_{v}(B(v))}{T(v)-B(v)}+\\
(1-2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})\cdot\frac{u_{v}(T(v))-u_{v}(B(v))}{T(v)-B(v)}.

We then follow the proof of Lemma
A.2
, which goes through some
slight modifications. For the case where
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}(h(v))<\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}(h(v))
, we are left with

\frac{d}{dv}\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h(v))=\frac{\partial}{\partial v}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}(T(v))-\frac{\partial}{\partial v}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}(B(v))

(A.10)

where

\frac{\partial}{\partial v}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}(h)={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial v}w_{v}(h)\right]+(1-2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial v}u_{v}(h)\right]={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}Q(v-h)+(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})hQ^{\prime}(v-h).

By construction,
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(T(v))={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(B(v))
, which means

(1-2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})Q(v-T(v))-(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})T(v)Q^{\prime}(v-T(v))=(1-2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})Q(v-B(v))-(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})B(v)Q^{\prime}(v-B(v)).

Substituting this equation into the previous, (
A.10
) becomes

\frac{d}{dv}\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{\prime}(h(v))=(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})\big[Q(v-T(v))-Q(v-B(v))\big]\leq 0,

completing the proof.

Proof of Proposition
7
.

We prove that for any market
x\notin O_{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}
,
x\notin O_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}^{\prime}}
. Denote by
u_{v}(h;{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})
the local information rate function with isoelastic costs:

u_{v}(h;{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})=h(v-h)^{\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}-1},

By Lemma
6
, with isoelastic costs,
u_{v}(h;{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})=\overline{u}_{v}(h;{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})
if and only if

h\leq\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}v.

This condition obviously becomes weaker as
\textstyle\mathchar 269
increases; hence, if it is not satisfied at
\textstyle\mathchar 269
, then it is also not satisfied by
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}^{\prime}<{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}
.

Now, take
v_{k}\in\supp(x)
such that
u_{v}^{\prime}(h_{k+1}^{x};{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})<u_{v}^{\prime}(h_{k}^{x};{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})
. Consider the function
h(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})
defined on
[v_{k},v_{k+1}]
which maintains
u^{\prime}_{v}(h(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}});{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})=u^{\prime}_{k}(h_{k}^{x};{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})
. To characterize
h
, first compute the scalar derivative:

u_{v}^{\prime}(h,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})=(v-h)^{\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}-1}\left(v-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}h\right).

Then, take the total derivative of this expression with respect to
v
when
h=h(v)
:

\frac{d}{dv}\Big[u_{v}^{\prime}(h(v);{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})\Big]=\left(\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}-1\right)(v-h(v))^{\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}-2}(1-h^{\prime}(v))\left(v-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}h(v)\right)+\\
(v-h(v))^{\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}-1}\left(1-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}h^{\prime}(v)\right).

The differential equation characterizing
h(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})
is:

\frac{d}{dv}\Big[u_{v}^{\prime}(h(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}});{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})\Big]=0\iff h^{\prime}(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})=\frac{(v-h(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}))-\frac{2-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}h(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})}{2(v-h(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}))-\frac{2-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}h(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})}.

(A.11)

Note that

h(v)\leq\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}v\implies h^{\prime}(v)\geq 0.

Furthermore, observe that (
A.11
) is increasing in
\textstyle\mathchar 269
. Since
x\notin O_{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}
,

h_{k+1}^{x}>h(v_{k+1};{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})\implies h_{k+1}^{x}-h_{k}^{x}>\int_{v_{k}}^{v_{k+1}}h^{\prime}(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}})\ dv>\int_{v_{k}}^{v_{k+1}}h^{\prime}(v,{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}^{\prime})\ dv.

(A.12)

Hence,
x\notin O_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}^{\prime}}
. The strict inclusion follows from observing that we can replace the first inequality of (
A.12
) with a weak inequality, so if
x
is on the border of
O_{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}
, then
x\notin O_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}^{\prime}}
.

Proof of Proposition
8
.

If
e=(+1,-1)
, then the problem is to maximize profits and minimize consumer surplus, which is achieved by first degree (perfect) price discrimination. So, it suffices to consider the case where
e_{1}=-1
. Rewrite the objective function as

-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}W+(e_{2}(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})U=U+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}W

where

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}\triangleq\frac{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}{e_{2}(1-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}})+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\in(-\infty,\infty).

Correspondingly, define the local objective

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}(h)=u_{k}(h)+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}w_{k}(h).

We can compute

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}^{\prime}(h)=Q(v_{k}-h)-(1+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}})hQ^{\prime}(v_{k}-h),

and

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}^{\prime\prime}(h)=(1+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}})hQ^{\prime\prime}(v_{k}-h)-(2+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}})Q^{\prime}(v_{k}-h).

We divide the analysis into three cases. First, if
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}\leq-2
, then
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{\prime\prime}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}(h)\geq 0
, meaning that the objective is convex everywhere. The concavification is then given by the line segment between 0 and
v_{k}
:

\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}(h)={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}\left(1-\frac{h}{v_{k}}\right)w_{k}(0).

This has derivative

\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}^{\prime}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}(h)=-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}{v_{k}}w_{k}(0)=-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}{v_{k}}\big(v_{k}Q(v_{k})-c(Q(v_{k}))\big).

This expression is increasing in
k
, so under (
39
) we have submodularity and the solution to the concavification bound is
D=D^{\ast}
, by an argument identical to that of Lemma
5
. The solution is implementable by putting as much weight as possible on the distribution where
h_{k}^{x}=v_{k}
, and then dividing the remaining mass into degenerate distributions with all weight on a single value.

Next, suppose
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}\geq-1
. We then have
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{\prime\prime}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}\leq 0
, and
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}
is concave everywhere. The rest of the analysis is identical to that of Proposition
5
;
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{\prime}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}(h)
is increasing in
v_{k}
, and hence we have both submodularity and implementability in segmentations with gapless support.

The final case is when
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}\in(-2,-1)
. Now,
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}
is increasing everywhere, but is concave at
h
if and only if

h\leq\frac{2+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}{1+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\cdot\frac{Q^{\prime}(v_{k}-h)}{Q^{\prime\prime}(v_{k}-h)}.

We claim that
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}
switches from concave to convex exactly once, then remains convex. A sufficient condition for this is that

\frac{Q^{\prime}(v_{k}-h)}{Q^{\prime\prime}(v_{k}-h)}\text{ is decreasing in }h\iff\sup_{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}\frac{Q^{\prime}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}})Q^{\prime\prime\prime}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}})}{(Q^{\prime\prime}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}))^{2}}\leq 1.

This is satisfied by the isoelastic cost function with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}\geq 2
. Thus:

\overline{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}(h)=\begin{cases}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}(h)&\text{if }h\leq\hat{h}_{k};\\
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}(\hat{h}_{k})&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}^{\prime}(\hat{h}_{k})=-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}(\hat{h}_{k})}{v_{k}-\hat{h}_{k}}.

We now claim that
\hat{h}_{k}
is increasing in
k
, and hence we have the single-crossing property used in the proof of Proposition
5
. Here, we work directly with the functional form:

(v_{k}-\hat{h}_{k})^{\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}}-\frac{1+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}\cdot\hat{h}_{k}(v_{k}-\hat{h}_{k})^{\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}-1}=-\frac{(\hat{h}_{k}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}v_{k})(v_{k}-\hat{h}_{k})^{\frac{1}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}/{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}(v_{k}-\hat{h}_{k})^{\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}}}{v_{k}-\hat{h}_{k}}.

This simplifies to

\hat{h}_{k}=\frac{(1+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}})-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}}{\frac{1+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}-1}-\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}}}v_{k}.

Importantly,
\hat{h}_{k}
only matters when it is positive and less than
v_{k}
, and when it is positive it must be an increasing function of
v_{k}
.

Lastly, we need to establish that submodularity holds. It is easy to verify that
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}^{\prime}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}
is increasing in
v_{k}
whenever
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}},k}^{\prime\prime}\leq 0
, and hence when
h_{k}^{\ast}\leq\hat{h}_{k}
we have submodularity. Otherwise, we need to compute the derivatives at
\hat{h}_{k}
:
